Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `int_sop_2` (precision INT19)
- computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
- output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`ax`, `ay`, `bx`, `by`) at one clock edge and `result` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `result` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `chainin` is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches `result` 2 cycles after it is applied. `chainout` is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value `result` loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before `result` shows it.
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by `reset`; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
- tie-off: `chainin` = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's `chainout` or a constant may drive it); `mode_sigs` = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, dla_like.small.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified dla_like.small under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 6.81328 ns on its one clock clk (I/O pads not timed) and uses 114 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 306. Its used logic-block area is 1.67592e+08 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 911396, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.04068e+15. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in dsp_block_16_8_false: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), then one adder chain of 12 carry stages, then one LUT, into a flip-flop with synchronous clear (resulta_tmp); wire 1.73 ns into the multiply, 0.96 into the adder, 1.39 into the LUT. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in a different dsp_block_16_8_false instance (endpoint group $sdff~5296, not path 1's $sdff~4939), is the same shape as path 1 — one hard multiply on a DSP tile, one adder chain of 15 carry stages, one LUT, into the resulta_tmp flip-flop, 6.808 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_dsp_block.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: dla_like.small.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T1
REGIONS: dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1166, dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1167
SHARED_REDUCTION: resulta_tmp in module dsp_block_16_8_false (dla_like.small.v:11745), i.e. ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg
BLOCKS: 72
TILES: 114
STAGE_NS: 6.20
MAPPING: one int_sop_2 instance inside dsp_block_16_8_false, replacing ax_reg/ay_reg/bx_reg/by_reg/resulta_tmp and the adder — .clk(clk), .reset(aclr), .mode_sigs(12'd0), .ax({2'b00, ax}), .ay({11'b0, ay}), .bx({2'b00, bx}), .by({11'b0, by}), .chainin(37'd0), .result(sop_result) with assign resulta = sop_result[23:0]; assign chainout = {40'b0, sop_result[23:0]};, .chainout() left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: the chainin input port of dsp_block_16_8_false is 0 on every cycle. dot_product_16_8_30_2 is the only module that instantiates dsp_block_16_8_false and it ties .chainin(64'd0) (dla_like.small.v:11687); the reference testbench also holds it at 0 (reg [63:0] chainin = 0;, tb_equiv_dsp_block.v:43, and the header note on line 3). The block's chainin is a dedicated chain input that may only be driven by another block's chainout or a constant, so it is tied to 37'd0 and the module's chainin port is left unread. ena is likewise tied to 1'b1 at that instantiation (line 11681) and is already not read by the module body, so the block needs no enable.

The edit. In dsp_block_16_8_false (dla_like.small.v:11696-11752), delete ax_reg, ay_reg, bx_reg, by_reg, resulta_tmp and the whole always @(posedge clk) block (11727-11747), and instantiate one int_sop_2 with the mapping above, declaring wire [36:0] sop_result;. Keep the module's port list unchanged. The dead wire [11:0] mode (11709-11710) is already unread in the baseline; leave it as it is. Nothing outside dsp_block_16_8_false changes. The module is instantiated once per dot_product_16_8_30_2, 6 per processing_element, 12 processing_elements, so the one edit yields 72 blocks.

Why it is correct.

  • Arithmetic. ax and bx are 16-bit, ay and by are 8-bit, all plain reg/unsigned, so zero-extension to the 18- and 19-bit ports is exact. With chainin = 0 the exact sum ax*ay + bx*by is at most 2*(65535*255) = 33,422,850 < 2^26, so the block's 37-bit result holds it exactly and result[23:0] equals the design's 24-bit truncation of the same sum. The design's RHS is evaluated at 64-bit width (because of the 64-bit chainin term) and then truncated on assignment to the 24-bit resulta_tmp, so there is no intermediate wrap to reproduce.
  • Registers. The design registers ax/ay/bx/by at one edge and loads resulta_tmp from those registers at the next: exactly the block's two-register shape, operands to result in 2 cycles. No clock enable is used (ena is unread).
  • Reset. aclr is synchronous and active high and clears the operand registers and resulta_tmp together; the block's reset clears the operand registers, the chainin register and result together. Driving reset with aclr reproduces it on every reset, not only at start-up: on the first edge after release the design loads resulta_tmp from cleared operand registers (0), and so does the block.
  • Power-up. The design's registers have no initial value either, and the rewrite adds no register of its own, so nothing relies on an initial value.
  • Visibility. Nothing inside the module is read elsewhere: ax_reg..by_reg and the two products are local; resulta_tmp leaves only through resulta and chainout, both driven from result[23:0]. chains_0 in dot_product_16_8_30_2 (line 11656) receives chainout and is not read further, and resulta reaches accumulator_24_30_3 only through o_result.
  • chainout timing. The design's chainout is the registered resulta_tmp, one cycle later than the block's combinational chainout, so the block's chainout pin is not used; chainout is driven from result, keeping its cycle.

Candidate regions left, and why. All sixteen winograd_dsp_16.$mul$...$733/$734(sum_in=winograd_transform_0/1) rows (112 products across 56 winograd_dsp_16 instances, 28 in each winograd_transform) are left. They share these reasons:

  • Truncation before the sum. resa_reg/resb_reg are 16-bit and the products ay_reg*coefa, by_reg*coefb are 16x16 truncated to 16 bits (lines 8966-8967) before leaving the module as resulta/resultb. The sum is formed in winograd_adder_16_20_4 at 20-bit width from those truncated 16-bit values (lines 8880-8894), so the carries out of bit 15 that the block's 37-bit sum of untruncated products would produce are not in the original. The block cannot reproduce the sum without re-truncating each product outside it, which defeats the rewrite.
  • Visibility. Each product is a module output (resulta, resultb of winograd_dsp_16) read in the parent as dsp_out_* (e.g. lines 9470-9471 feeding 9807/9809), so each truncated product must stay visible outside any block.
  • Clock enable on the sum. winograd_adder_16_20_4 is instantiated with .clken(calculate_2) (lines 7797, 9805); its pipeline registers hold when the enable is low. The block has no clock enable and no path around its result register, so its result register cannot stand in for an enabled register.
  • Reset. Every winograd_dsp_16 is instantiated with .aclr(1'b0) (e.g. line 9465), so its registers are never cleared and must behave as if they held 0 from power-up. Every register of the block is X until its first clear by reset, and a rewrite may not rely on an added register's initial value, so no reset can be derived that makes the block match from power-up.
  • Shape and cost. There is one register stage between the products and the sum (resa_reg/resb_reg), and the sums are trees of 4 or 5 products mixed with non-product terms such as {feature_reg_0_0_1[11:0], 4'b0000} (line 9806); the block's shape is two products plus a chain term with no register between them. Converting them would also cost tiles: these 112 products currently pack two to a tile, while each block takes a tile alone.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite adds 72 blocks, one per dsp_block_16_8_false instance. The baseline packs 224 multiply primitives on 114 tiles (110 with two, 4 with one); the 144 multiplies of these 72 modules are two per tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, i.e. 72 tiles, and the winograd multiplies that remain on tiles occupy the other 42. After the rewrite each of the 72 blocks owns a tile alone and the winograd side is untouched, so the design uses 72 + 42 = 114 tiles, unchanged against the baseline (112 if the winograd side repacks its 4 single-occupancy tiles). DSP area is therefore flat and the area win is entirely soft logic.

Blocks in series. No chaining: chainin is tied to 0 on every block, because each dsp_block_16_8_false is a standalone two-product sum whose result is read by its own accumulator_24_30_3, so no two blocks' sums combine. The longest series of blocks in any stage is one, and that stage is input register clock-to-Q + 2.140 + output register setup = 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's slowest stage of 6.81328 ns. The two stages the rewrite touches at the block's edges are also short: into the block, f_pipeline_*/w_pipeline_* clock-to-Q + a register-to-first-block wire + setup = 0.060 + 1.001..2.461 + 0.019 = 1.08..2.54 ns; out of the block, result clock-to-Q 0.060 plus the wire and the sign-extend LUT into in_reg of accumulator_24_30_3 (lines 11986-11990), which is the same consumer path the soft resulta_tmp fed and whose source clock-to-Q is 0.060 either way, so that stage is unchanged.

Soft logic leaving, and which stage. Out of each of the 72 instances go the 48 operand flip-flops (ax_reg 16, ay_reg 8, bx_reg 16, by_reg 8), the 24-bit adder that sums the two products (the carry chain of 12 stages plus the LUT named on the critical path) and the 24-bit resulta_tmp flip-flop with synchronous clear: about 5184 flip-flops and roughly 1700 adder/LUT cells across the design. That is exactly the baseline's slowest stage — path 1 (endpoint group $sdff~4939, 6.81328 ns: wire 1.73 into the multiply, 0.96 into the adder chain of 12, 1.39 into the LUT, into resulta_tmp) — and also exactly path 2 (endpoint group $sdff~5296, 6.808 ns, the same shape with 15 carry stages in another dsp_block_16_8_false instance). Both live in this module, and the edit is to the module, so all 72 instances of that stage collapse to the 2.219 ns inside-block stage at once; this is the reason the rewrite is worth doing even though path 2 limits what shortening one instance would buy.

Resulting slowest stage. With every stage of this shape gone, the longest stage left is one the rewrite does not touch: the winograd_dsp_16 multiply, coefa/ay_reg clock-to-Q into a hard multiply on a tile and straight into the soft flip-flop resa_reg with no soft adder between (lines 8966-8967), estimated at 0.060 + 1.835 (register to first block, median on this design) + 2.140 (block) + 2.147 (last block to register, the one sample on this design) + 0.019 = 6.20 ns. Every other remaining path is a single pipelined adder, xor or multiplexer level per register stage (winograd_adder_16_20_4 8880-8894, inverse_winograd_adder_30_3 2113-2119, accumulator_24_30_3 11986-11998, pipelined_xor_tree_16), all well under that. So the rewrite is expected to take the period from 6.81328 ns to about 6.20 ns, cut roughly 5000 flip-flops and 1700 soft adder cells (on the order of a 10% drop in used logic-block area at an unchanged tile count) and shorten wirelength by removing the 24-bit product buses that currently run from the DSP tiles into the soft adder chains, which lowers the area x delay x wirelength objective below the baseline's 1.04068e+15.

Register stages, before and after, for the converted region. Before: ax/ay/bx/by to ax_reg/ay_reg/bx_reg/by_reg is one register stage, then combinational multiply-add into resulta_tmp is the second; operands reach resulta in 2 cycles, and the products are registered 0 stages before the sum. After: the block's input register is the first stage and its result register the second, with the two products and their sum combinational between them; operands reach resulta in 2 cycles, unchanged. No register is moved, added or removed anywhere in the design.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if winograd_dsp_16 published its products untruncated (36-bit resulta/resultb instead of 16-bit) and winograd_adder_16_20_4 dropped its clken or gated data rather than the registers, the 112 winograd products could be regrouped two at a time into chained int_sop_2 blocks, with the blocks' chainout-to-chainin chains replacing the first two levels of each 16-input adder tree.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The design

The design source is dla_like.small.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T1

PLAN:

ASSUMES: the chainin input port of dsp_block_16_8_false is 0 on every cycle. dot_product_16_8_30_2 is the only module that instantiates dsp_block_16_8_false and it ties .chainin(64'd0) (dla_like.small.v:11687); the reference testbench also holds it at 0 (reg [63:0] chainin = 0;, tb_equiv_dsp_block.v:43, and the header note on line 3). The block's chainin is a dedicated chain input that may only be driven by another block's chainout or a constant, so it is tied to 37'd0 and the module's chainin port is left unread. ena is likewise tied to 1'b1 at that instantiation (line 11681) and is already not read by the module body, so the block needs no enable.

The edit. In dsp_block_16_8_false (dla_like.small.v:11696-11752), delete ax_reg, ay_reg, bx_reg, by_reg, resulta_tmp and the whole always @(posedge clk) block (11727-11747), and instantiate one int_sop_2 with the mapping above, declaring wire [36:0] sop_result;. Keep the module's port list unchanged. The dead wire [11:0] mode (11709-11710) is already unread in the baseline; leave it as it is. Nothing outside dsp_block_16_8_false changes. The module is instantiated once per dot_product_16_8_30_2, 6 per processing_element, 12 processing_elements, so the one edit yields 72 blocks.

Why it is correct.

  • Arithmetic. ax and bx are 16-bit, ay and by are 8-bit, all plain reg/unsigned, so zero-extension to the 18- and 19-bit ports is exact. With chainin = 0 the exact sum ax*ay + bx*by is at most 2*(65535*255) = 33,422,850 < 2^26, so the block's 37-bit result holds it exactly and result[23:0] equals the design's 24-bit truncation of the same sum. The design's RHS is evaluated at 64-bit width (because of the 64-bit chainin term) and then truncated on assignment to the 24-bit resulta_tmp, so there is no intermediate wrap to reproduce.
  • Registers. The design registers ax/ay/bx/by at one edge and loads resulta_tmp from those registers at the next: exactly the block's two-register shape, operands to result in 2 cycles. No clock enable is used (ena is unread).
  • Reset. aclr is synchronous and active high and clears the operand registers and resulta_tmp together; the block's reset clears the operand registers, the chainin register and result together. Driving reset with aclr reproduces it on every reset, not only at start-up: on the first edge after release the design loads resulta_tmp from cleared operand registers (0), and so does the block.
  • Power-up. The design's registers have no initial value either, and the rewrite adds no register of its own, so nothing relies on an initial value.
  • Visibility. Nothing inside the module is read elsewhere: ax_reg..by_reg and the two products are local; resulta_tmp leaves only through resulta and chainout, both driven from result[23:0]. chains_0 in dot_product_16_8_30_2 (line 11656) receives chainout and is not read further, and resulta reaches accumulator_24_30_3 only through o_result.
  • chainout timing. The design's chainout is the registered resulta_tmp, one cycle later than the block's combinational chainout, so the block's chainout pin is not used; chainout is driven from result, keeping its cycle.

Candidate regions left, and why. All sixteen winograd_dsp_16.$mul$...$733/$734(sum_in=winograd_transform_0/1) rows (112 products across 56 winograd_dsp_16 instances, 28 in each winograd_transform) are left. They share these reasons:

  • Truncation before the sum. resa_reg/resb_reg are 16-bit and the products ay_reg*coefa, by_reg*coefb are 16x16 truncated to 16 bits (lines 8966-8967) before leaving the module as resulta/resultb. The sum is formed in winograd_adder_16_20_4 at 20-bit width from those truncated 16-bit values (lines 8880-8894), so the carries out of bit 15 that the block's 37-bit sum of untruncated products would produce are not in the original. The block cannot reproduce the sum without re-truncating each product outside it, which defeats the rewrite.
  • Visibility. Each product is a module output (resulta, resultb of winograd_dsp_16) read in the parent as dsp_out_* (e.g. lines 9470-9471 feeding 9807/9809), so each truncated product must stay visible outside any block.
  • Clock enable on the sum. winograd_adder_16_20_4 is instantiated with .clken(calculate_2) (lines 7797, 9805); its pipeline registers hold when the enable is low. The block has no clock enable and no path around its result register, so its result register cannot stand in for an enabled register.
  • Reset. Every winograd_dsp_16 is instantiated with .aclr(1'b0) (e.g. line 9465), so its registers are never cleared and must behave as if they held 0 from power-up. Every register of the block is X until its first clear by reset, and a rewrite may not rely on an added register's initial value, so no reset can be derived that makes the block match from power-up.
  • Shape and cost. There is one register stage between the products and the sum (resa_reg/resb_reg), and the sums are trees of 4 or 5 products mixed with non-product terms such as {feature_reg_0_0_1[11:0], 4'b0000} (line 9806); the block's shape is two products plus a chain term with no register between them. Converting them would also cost tiles: these 112 products currently pack two to a tile, while each block takes a tile alone.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite adds 72 blocks, one per dsp_block_16_8_false instance. The baseline packs 224 multiply primitives on 114 tiles (110 with two, 4 with one); the 144 multiplies of these 72 modules are two per tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, i.e. 72 tiles, and the winograd multiplies that remain on tiles occupy the other 42. After the rewrite each of the 72 blocks owns a tile alone and the winograd side is untouched, so the design uses 72 + 42 = 114 tiles, unchanged against the baseline (112 if the winograd side repacks its 4 single-occupancy tiles). DSP area is therefore flat and the area win is entirely soft logic.

Blocks in series. No chaining: chainin is tied to 0 on every block, because each dsp_block_16_8_false is a standalone two-product sum whose result is read by its own accumulator_24_30_3, so no two blocks' sums combine. The longest series of blocks in any stage is one, and that stage is input register clock-to-Q + 2.140 + output register setup = 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's slowest stage of 6.81328 ns. The two stages the rewrite touches at the block's edges are also short: into the block, f_pipeline_*/w_pipeline_* clock-to-Q + a register-to-first-block wire + setup = 0.060 + 1.001..2.461 + 0.019 = 1.08..2.54 ns; out of the block, result clock-to-Q 0.060 plus the wire and the sign-extend LUT into in_reg of accumulator_24_30_3 (lines 11986-11990), which is the same consumer path the soft resulta_tmp fed and whose source clock-to-Q is 0.060 either way, so that stage is unchanged.

Soft logic leaving, and which stage. Out of each of the 72 instances go the 48 operand flip-flops (ax_reg 16, ay_reg 8, bx_reg 16, by_reg 8), the 24-bit adder that sums the two products (the carry chain of 12 stages plus the LUT named on the critical path) and the 24-bit resulta_tmp flip-flop with synchronous clear: about 5184 flip-flops and roughly 1700 adder/LUT cells across the design. That is exactly the baseline's slowest stage — path 1 (endpoint group $sdff~4939, 6.81328 ns: wire 1.73 into the multiply, 0.96 into the adder chain of 12, 1.39 into the LUT, into resulta_tmp) — and also exactly path 2 (endpoint group $sdff~5296, 6.808 ns, the same shape with 15 carry stages in another dsp_block_16_8_false instance). Both live in this module, and the edit is to the module, so all 72 instances of that stage collapse to the 2.219 ns inside-block stage at once; this is the reason the rewrite is worth doing even though path 2 limits what shortening one instance would buy.

Resulting slowest stage. With every stage of this shape gone, the longest stage left is one the rewrite does not touch: the winograd_dsp_16 multiply, coefa/ay_reg clock-to-Q into a hard multiply on a tile and straight into the soft flip-flop resa_reg with no soft adder between (lines 8966-8967), estimated at 0.060 + 1.835 (register to first block, median on this design) + 2.140 (block) + 2.147 (last block to register, the one sample on this design) + 0.019 = 6.20 ns. Every other remaining path is a single pipelined adder, xor or multiplexer level per register stage (winograd_adder_16_20_4 8880-8894, inverse_winograd_adder_30_3 2113-2119, accumulator_24_30_3 11986-11998, pipelined_xor_tree_16), all well under that. So the rewrite is expected to take the period from 6.81328 ns to about 6.20 ns, cut roughly 5000 flip-flops and 1700 soft adder cells (on the order of a 10% drop in used logic-block area at an unchanged tile count) and shorten wirelength by removing the 24-bit product buses that currently run from the DSP tiles into the soft adder chains, which lowers the area x delay x wirelength objective below the baseline's 1.04068e+15.

Register stages, before and after, for the converted region. Before: ax/ay/bx/by to ax_reg/ay_reg/bx_reg/by_reg is one register stage, then combinational multiply-add into resulta_tmp is the second; operands reach resulta in 2 cycles, and the products are registered 0 stages before the sum. After: the block's input register is the first stage and its result register the second, with the two products and their sum combinational between them; operands reach resulta in 2 cycles, unchanged. No register is moved, added or removed anywhere in the design.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if winograd_dsp_16 published its products untruncated (36-bit resulta/resultb instead of 16-bit) and winograd_adder_16_20_4 dropped its clken or gated data rather than the registers, the 112 winograd products could be regrouped two at a time into chained int_sop_2 blocks, with the blocks' chainout-to-chainin chains replacing the first two levels of each 16-input adder tree.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_dsp_block.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: dla_like.small.v

response

I'll read the design source and reference files before judging.

APPROVE

Verified against dla_like.small.v and sim_reference/:

  • Conversion region. dsp_block_16_8_false (11696–11752): ax[15:0]/bx[15:0] → 18-bit, ay[7:0]/by[7:0] → 19-bit zero-extensions are exact; resulta_tmp <= ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg + chainin (11745) is evaluated at 64 bits and truncated to 24, so result[23:0] reproduces it; resulta (11748) and chainout = {40'b0, resulta_tmp} (11749) are the only readers, and driving both from result keeps chainout's registered cycle (the block's combinational chainout pin is correctly left unused). Two-register shape and the synchronous active-high aclr clearing operands and result together (11732–11747) match the contract's registers and reset, including mid-run resets. ena is unread in the body; mode (11709–11710) is dead.
  • ASSUMES. chainin tied 64'd0 at the sole instantiation (11687, and dsp_block_16_8_false is instantiated only there), ena tied 1'b1 (11681), chains_0 declared at 11656 and read nowhere else; the testbench fixes chainin = 0 (tb line 43, header line 3). X-at-power-up is covered: the tb asserts aclr for RESET_CYCLES before comparing.
  • Counts. 12 processing_element (732–1161) × 6 dot_product_16_8_30_2 (11431–11489) = 72 blocks; 56 winograd_dsp_16 instances, 28 per transform, all with .aclr(1'b0) (56 occurrences).
  • Left regions. Decisive and verified: resa_reg/resb_reg truncate the products to 16 bits before the sum (8913–8915, 8966–8967) and each truncated product leaves as resulta/resultb and is read as dsp_out_* (9470–9471 → 9807/9809), so no block can both hold the sum and expose the two truncated products.
  • Required items. Blocks (72) and tiles (114), longest block series (one, 2.219 ns vs 6.81328 ns baseline stage), soft logic leaving the slowest stage, and register stages before/after (2 → 2) are all stated.

One inaccuracy that does not change the plan or the rewrite: winograd_adder_16_20_4 declares clken (8842) and it is connected (7797, 9805), but the body's always @(posedge clock) (8879–8895) ignores it, so those pipeline registers do not hold when calculate_2 is low. The region is still correctly left on the truncation and visibility grounds above.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt
Task

Edit dla_like.small.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1166, dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1167. The plan names their shared sum as 'resulta_tmp in module dsp_block_16_8_false (dla_like.small.v:11745), i.e. ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg'. In the design their products are registered 0 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh dla_like.small.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T1
REGIONS: dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1166, dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1167
SHARED_REDUCTION: resulta_tmp in module dsp_block_16_8_false (dla_like.small.v:11745), i.e. ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg
BLOCKS: 72
TILES: 114
STAGE_NS: 6.20
MAPPING: one int_sop_2 instance inside dsp_block_16_8_false, replacing ax_reg/ay_reg/bx_reg/by_reg/resulta_tmp and the adder — .clk(clk), .reset(aclr), .mode_sigs(12'd0), .ax({2'b00, ax}), .ay({11'b0, ay}), .bx({2'b00, bx}), .by({11'b0, by}), .chainin(37'd0), .result(sop_result) with assign resulta = sop_result[23:0]; assign chainout = {40'b0, sop_result[23:0]};, .chainout() left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: the chainin input port of dsp_block_16_8_false is 0 on every cycle. dot_product_16_8_30_2 is the only module that instantiates dsp_block_16_8_false and it ties .chainin(64'd0) (dla_like.small.v:11687); the reference testbench also holds it at 0 (reg [63:0] chainin = 0;, tb_equiv_dsp_block.v:43, and the header note on line 3). The block's chainin is a dedicated chain input that may only be driven by another block's chainout or a constant, so it is tied to 37'd0 and the module's chainin port is left unread. ena is likewise tied to 1'b1 at that instantiation (line 11681) and is already not read by the module body, so the block needs no enable.

The edit. In dsp_block_16_8_false (dla_like.small.v:11696-11752), delete ax_reg, ay_reg, bx_reg, by_reg, resulta_tmp and the whole always @(posedge clk) block (11727-11747), and instantiate one int_sop_2 with the mapping above, declaring wire [36:0] sop_result;. Keep the module's port list unchanged. The dead wire [11:0] mode (11709-11710) is already unread in the baseline; leave it as it is. Nothing outside dsp_block_16_8_false changes. The module is instantiated once per dot_product_16_8_30_2, 6 per processing_element, 12 processing_elements, so the one edit yields 72 blocks.

Why it is correct.

  • Arithmetic. ax and bx are 16-bit, ay and by are 8-bit, all plain reg/unsigned, so zero-extension to the 18- and 19-bit ports is exact. With chainin = 0 the exact sum ax*ay + bx*by is at most 2*(65535*255) = 33,422,850 < 2^26, so the block's 37-bit result holds it exactly and result[23:0] equals the design's 24-bit truncation of the same sum. The design's RHS is evaluated at 64-bit width (because of the 64-bit chainin term) and then truncated on assignment to the 24-bit resulta_tmp, so there is no intermediate wrap to reproduce.
  • Registers. The design registers ax/ay/bx/by at one edge and loads resulta_tmp from those registers at the next: exactly the block's two-register shape, operands to result in 2 cycles. No clock enable is used (ena is unread).
  • Reset. aclr is synchronous and active high and clears the operand registers and resulta_tmp together; the block's reset clears the operand registers, the chainin register and result together. Driving reset with aclr reproduces it on every reset, not only at start-up: on the first edge after release the design loads resulta_tmp from cleared operand registers (0), and so does the block.
  • Power-up. The design's registers have no initial value either, and the rewrite adds no register of its own, so nothing relies on an initial value.
  • Visibility. Nothing inside the module is read elsewhere: ax_reg..by_reg and the two products are local; resulta_tmp leaves only through resulta and chainout, both driven from result[23:0]. chains_0 in dot_product_16_8_30_2 (line 11656) receives chainout and is not read further, and resulta reaches accumulator_24_30_3 only through o_result.
  • chainout timing. The design's chainout is the registered resulta_tmp, one cycle later than the block's combinational chainout, so the block's chainout pin is not used; chainout is driven from result, keeping its cycle.

Candidate regions left, and why. All sixteen winograd_dsp_16.$mul$...$733/$734(sum_in=winograd_transform_0/1) rows (112 products across 56 winograd_dsp_16 instances, 28 in each winograd_transform) are left. They share these reasons:

  • Truncation before the sum. resa_reg/resb_reg are 16-bit and the products ay_reg*coefa, by_reg*coefb are 16x16 truncated to 16 bits (lines 8966-8967) before leaving the module as resulta/resultb. The sum is formed in winograd_adder_16_20_4 at 20-bit width from those truncated 16-bit values (lines 8880-8894), so the carries out of bit 15 that the block's 37-bit sum of untruncated products would produce are not in the original. The block cannot reproduce the sum without re-truncating each product outside it, which defeats the rewrite.
  • Visibility. Each product is a module output (resulta, resultb of winograd_dsp_16) read in the parent as dsp_out_* (e.g. lines 9470-9471 feeding 9807/9809), so each truncated product must stay visible outside any block.
  • Clock enable on the sum. winograd_adder_16_20_4 is instantiated with .clken(calculate_2) (lines 7797, 9805); its pipeline registers hold when the enable is low. The block has no clock enable and no path around its result register, so its result register cannot stand in for an enabled register.
  • Reset. Every winograd_dsp_16 is instantiated with .aclr(1'b0) (e.g. line 9465), so its registers are never cleared and must behave as if they held 0 from power-up. Every register of the block is X until its first clear by reset, and a rewrite may not rely on an added register's initial value, so no reset can be derived that makes the block match from power-up.
  • Shape and cost. There is one register stage between the products and the sum (resa_reg/resb_reg), and the sums are trees of 4 or 5 products mixed with non-product terms such as {feature_reg_0_0_1[11:0], 4'b0000} (line 9806); the block's shape is two products plus a chain term with no register between them. Converting them would also cost tiles: these 112 products currently pack two to a tile, while each block takes a tile alone.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite adds 72 blocks, one per dsp_block_16_8_false instance. The baseline packs 224 multiply primitives on 114 tiles (110 with two, 4 with one); the 144 multiplies of these 72 modules are two per tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, i.e. 72 tiles, and the winograd multiplies that remain on tiles occupy the other 42. After the rewrite each of the 72 blocks owns a tile alone and the winograd side is untouched, so the design uses 72 + 42 = 114 tiles, unchanged against the baseline (112 if the winograd side repacks its 4 single-occupancy tiles). DSP area is therefore flat and the area win is entirely soft logic.

Blocks in series. No chaining: chainin is tied to 0 on every block, because each dsp_block_16_8_false is a standalone two-product sum whose result is read by its own accumulator_24_30_3, so no two blocks' sums combine. The longest series of blocks in any stage is one, and that stage is input register clock-to-Q + 2.140 + output register setup = 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's slowest stage of 6.81328 ns. The two stages the rewrite touches at the block's edges are also short: into the block, f_pipeline_*/w_pipeline_* clock-to-Q + a register-to-first-block wire + setup = 0.060 + 1.001..2.461 + 0.019 = 1.08..2.54 ns; out of the block, result clock-to-Q 0.060 plus the wire and the sign-extend LUT into in_reg of accumulator_24_30_3 (lines 11986-11990), which is the same consumer path the soft resulta_tmp fed and whose source clock-to-Q is 0.060 either way, so that stage is unchanged.

Soft logic leaving, and which stage. Out of each of the 72 instances go the 48 operand flip-flops (ax_reg 16, ay_reg 8, bx_reg 16, by_reg 8), the 24-bit adder that sums the two products (the carry chain of 12 stages plus the LUT named on the critical path) and the 24-bit resulta_tmp flip-flop with synchronous clear: about 5184 flip-flops and roughly 1700 adder/LUT cells across the design. That is exactly the baseline's slowest stage — path 1 (endpoint group $sdff~4939, 6.81328 ns: wire 1.73 into the multiply, 0.96 into the adder chain of 12, 1.39 into the LUT, into resulta_tmp) — and also exactly path 2 (endpoint group $sdff~5296, 6.808 ns, the same shape with 15 carry stages in another dsp_block_16_8_false instance). Both live in this module, and the edit is to the module, so all 72 instances of that stage collapse to the 2.219 ns inside-block stage at once; this is the reason the rewrite is worth doing even though path 2 limits what shortening one instance would buy.

Resulting slowest stage. With every stage of this shape gone, the longest stage left is one the rewrite does not touch: the winograd_dsp_16 multiply, coefa/ay_reg clock-to-Q into a hard multiply on a tile and straight into the soft flip-flop resa_reg with no soft adder between (lines 8966-8967), estimated at 0.060 + 1.835 (register to first block, median on this design) + 2.140 (block) + 2.147 (last block to register, the one sample on this design) + 0.019 = 6.20 ns. Every other remaining path is a single pipelined adder, xor or multiplexer level per register stage (winograd_adder_16_20_4 8880-8894, inverse_winograd_adder_30_3 2113-2119, accumulator_24_30_3 11986-11998, pipelined_xor_tree_16), all well under that. So the rewrite is expected to take the period from 6.81328 ns to about 6.20 ns, cut roughly 5000 flip-flops and 1700 soft adder cells (on the order of a 10% drop in used logic-block area at an unchanged tile count) and shorten wirelength by removing the 24-bit product buses that currently run from the DSP tiles into the soft adder chains, which lowers the area x delay x wirelength objective below the baseline's 1.04068e+15.

Register stages, before and after, for the converted region. Before: ax/ay/bx/by to ax_reg/ay_reg/bx_reg/by_reg is one register stage, then combinational multiply-add into resulta_tmp is the second; operands reach resulta in 2 cycles, and the products are registered 0 stages before the sum. After: the block's input register is the first stage and its result register the second, with the two products and their sum combinational between them; operands reach resulta in 2 cycles, unchanged. No register is moved, added or removed anywhere in the design.

Simulation

The testbench (tb_equiv_dsp_block.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/int_sop_2.v"

output
1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule
51

1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
3	# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
4	# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
5	# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
6	# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
7	# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
8	# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
9	# of the target block's name fails this check.
10	# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
11	# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
12	# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
13	# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
14	set -u
15	f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
16	here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
17	lib=""
18	[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
19	[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
20	target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
21	if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
22	  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
23	  exit 1
24	fi
25	out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
26	rc=$?
27	if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
28	  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
29	  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
30	  echo "$out" | tail -10
31	  exit 1
32	fi
33	echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
34	echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
35	echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
36	                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
37	                   on {on=0}
38	                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The design

The file dla_like.small.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

Output

  • If REJECT: the rule, the lines, what is wrong.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T1
REGIONS: dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1166, dsp_block_16_8_false.$mul$dla_like.small.v:11745$1167
SHARED_REDUCTION: resulta_tmp in module dsp_block_16_8_false (dla_like.small.v:11745), i.e. ax_reg*ay_reg + bx_reg*by_reg
BLOCKS: 72
TILES: 114
STAGE_NS: 6.20
MAPPING: one int_sop_2 instance inside dsp_block_16_8_false, replacing ax_reg/ay_reg/bx_reg/by_reg/resulta_tmp and the adder — .clk(clk), .reset(aclr), .mode_sigs(12'd0), .ax({2'b00, ax}), .ay({11'b0, ay}), .bx({2'b00, bx}), .by({11'b0, by}), .chainin(37'd0), .result(sop_result) with assign resulta = sop_result[23:0]; assign chainout = {40'b0, sop_result[23:0]};, .chainout() left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: the chainin input port of dsp_block_16_8_false is 0 on every cycle. dot_product_16_8_30_2 is the only module that instantiates dsp_block_16_8_false and it ties .chainin(64'd0) (dla_like.small.v:11687); the reference testbench also holds it at 0 (reg [63:0] chainin = 0;, tb_equiv_dsp_block.v:43, and the header note on line 3). The block's chainin is a dedicated chain input that may only be driven by another block's chainout or a constant, so it is tied to 37'd0 and the module's chainin port is left unread. ena is likewise tied to 1'b1 at that instantiation (line 11681) and is already not read by the module body, so the block needs no enable.

The edit. In dsp_block_16_8_false (dla_like.small.v:11696-11752), delete ax_reg, ay_reg, bx_reg, by_reg, resulta_tmp and the whole always @(posedge clk) block (11727-11747), and instantiate one int_sop_2 with the mapping above, declaring wire [36:0] sop_result;. Keep the module's port list unchanged. The dead wire [11:0] mode (11709-11710) is already unread in the baseline; leave it as it is. Nothing outside dsp_block_16_8_false changes. The module is instantiated once per dot_product_16_8_30_2, 6 per processing_element, 12 processing_elements, so the one edit yields 72 blocks.

Why it is correct.

  • Arithmetic. ax and bx are 16-bit, ay and by are 8-bit, all plain reg/unsigned, so zero-extension to the 18- and 19-bit ports is exact. With chainin = 0 the exact sum ax*ay + bx*by is at most 2*(65535*255) = 33,422,850 < 2^26, so the block's 37-bit result holds it exactly and result[23:0] equals the design's 24-bit truncation of the same sum. The design's RHS is evaluated at 64-bit width (because of the 64-bit chainin term) and then truncated on assignment to the 24-bit resulta_tmp, so there is no intermediate wrap to reproduce.
  • Registers. The design registers ax/ay/bx/by at one edge and loads resulta_tmp from those registers at the next: exactly the block's two-register shape, operands to result in 2 cycles. No clock enable is used (ena is unread).
  • Reset. aclr is synchronous and active high and clears the operand registers and resulta_tmp together; the block's reset clears the operand registers, the chainin register and result together. Driving reset with aclr reproduces it on every reset, not only at start-up: on the first edge after release the design loads resulta_tmp from cleared operand registers (0), and so does the block.
  • Power-up. The design's registers have no initial value either, and the rewrite adds no register of its own, so nothing relies on an initial value.
  • Visibility. Nothing inside the module is read elsewhere: ax_reg..by_reg and the two products are local; resulta_tmp leaves only through resulta and chainout, both driven from result[23:0]. chains_0 in dot_product_16_8_30_2 (line 11656) receives chainout and is not read further, and resulta reaches accumulator_24_30_3 only through o_result.
  • chainout timing. The design's chainout is the registered resulta_tmp, one cycle later than the block's combinational chainout, so the block's chainout pin is not used; chainout is driven from result, keeping its cycle.

Candidate regions left, and why. All sixteen winograd_dsp_16.$mul$...$733/$734(sum_in=winograd_transform_0/1) rows (112 products across 56 winograd_dsp_16 instances, 28 in each winograd_transform) are left. They share these reasons:

  • Truncation before the sum. resa_reg/resb_reg are 16-bit and the products ay_reg*coefa, by_reg*coefb are 16x16 truncated to 16 bits (lines 8966-8967) before leaving the module as resulta/resultb. The sum is formed in winograd_adder_16_20_4 at 20-bit width from those truncated 16-bit values (lines 8880-8894), so the carries out of bit 15 that the block's 37-bit sum of untruncated products would produce are not in the original. The block cannot reproduce the sum without re-truncating each product outside it, which defeats the rewrite.
  • Visibility. Each product is a module output (resulta, resultb of winograd_dsp_16) read in the parent as dsp_out_* (e.g. lines 9470-9471 feeding 9807/9809), so each truncated product must stay visible outside any block.
  • Clock enable on the sum. winograd_adder_16_20_4 is instantiated with .clken(calculate_2) (lines 7797, 9805); its pipeline registers hold when the enable is low. The block has no clock enable and no path around its result register, so its result register cannot stand in for an enabled register.
  • Reset. Every winograd_dsp_16 is instantiated with .aclr(1'b0) (e.g. line 9465), so its registers are never cleared and must behave as if they held 0 from power-up. Every register of the block is X until its first clear by reset, and a rewrite may not rely on an added register's initial value, so no reset can be derived that makes the block match from power-up.
  • Shape and cost. There is one register stage between the products and the sum (resa_reg/resb_reg), and the sums are trees of 4 or 5 products mixed with non-product terms such as {feature_reg_0_0_1[11:0], 4'b0000} (line 9806); the block's shape is two products plus a chain term with no register between them. Converting them would also cost tiles: these 112 products currently pack two to a tile, while each block takes a tile alone.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite adds 72 blocks, one per dsp_block_16_8_false instance. The baseline packs 224 multiply primitives on 114 tiles (110 with two, 4 with one); the 144 multiplies of these 72 modules are two per tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, i.e. 72 tiles, and the winograd multiplies that remain on tiles occupy the other 42. After the rewrite each of the 72 blocks owns a tile alone and the winograd side is untouched, so the design uses 72 + 42 = 114 tiles, unchanged against the baseline (112 if the winograd side repacks its 4 single-occupancy tiles). DSP area is therefore flat and the area win is entirely soft logic.

Blocks in series. No chaining: chainin is tied to 0 on every block, because each dsp_block_16_8_false is a standalone two-product sum whose result is read by its own accumulator_24_30_3, so no two blocks' sums combine. The longest series of blocks in any stage is one, and that stage is input register clock-to-Q + 2.140 + output register setup = 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline's slowest stage of 6.81328 ns. The two stages the rewrite touches at the block's edges are also short: into the block, f_pipeline_*/w_pipeline_* clock-to-Q + a register-to-first-block wire + setup = 0.060 + 1.001..2.461 + 0.019 = 1.08..2.54 ns; out of the block, result clock-to-Q 0.060 plus the wire and the sign-extend LUT into in_reg of accumulator_24_30_3 (lines 11986-11990), which is the same consumer path the soft resulta_tmp fed and whose source clock-to-Q is 0.060 either way, so that stage is unchanged.

Soft logic leaving, and which stage. Out of each of the 72 instances go the 48 operand flip-flops (ax_reg 16, ay_reg 8, bx_reg 16, by_reg 8), the 24-bit adder that sums the two products (the carry chain of 12 stages plus the LUT named on the critical path) and the 24-bit resulta_tmp flip-flop with synchronous clear: about 5184 flip-flops and roughly 1700 adder/LUT cells across the design. That is exactly the baseline's slowest stage — path 1 (endpoint group $sdff~4939, 6.81328 ns: wire 1.73 into the multiply, 0.96 into the adder chain of 12, 1.39 into the LUT, into resulta_tmp) — and also exactly path 2 (endpoint group $sdff~5296, 6.808 ns, the same shape with 15 carry stages in another dsp_block_16_8_false instance). Both live in this module, and the edit is to the module, so all 72 instances of that stage collapse to the 2.219 ns inside-block stage at once; this is the reason the rewrite is worth doing even though path 2 limits what shortening one instance would buy.

Resulting slowest stage. With every stage of this shape gone, the longest stage left is one the rewrite does not touch: the winograd_dsp_16 multiply, coefa/ay_reg clock-to-Q into a hard multiply on a tile and straight into the soft flip-flop resa_reg with no soft adder between (lines 8966-8967), estimated at 0.060 + 1.835 (register to first block, median on this design) + 2.140 (block) + 2.147 (last block to register, the one sample on this design) + 0.019 = 6.20 ns. Every other remaining path is a single pipelined adder, xor or multiplexer level per register stage (winograd_adder_16_20_4 8880-8894, inverse_winograd_adder_30_3 2113-2119, accumulator_24_30_3 11986-11998, pipelined_xor_tree_16), all well under that. So the rewrite is expected to take the period from 6.81328 ns to about 6.20 ns, cut roughly 5000 flip-flops and 1700 soft adder cells (on the order of a 10% drop in used logic-block area at an unchanged tile count) and shorten wirelength by removing the 24-bit product buses that currently run from the DSP tiles into the soft adder chains, which lowers the area x delay x wirelength objective below the baseline's 1.04068e+15.

Register stages, before and after, for the converted region. Before: ax/ay/bx/by to ax_reg/ay_reg/bx_reg/by_reg is one register stage, then combinational multiply-add into resulta_tmp is the second; operands reach resulta in 2 cycles, and the products are registered 0 stages before the sum. After: the block's input register is the first stage and its result register the second, with the two products and their sum combinational between them; operands reach resulta in 2 cycles, unchanged. No register is moved, added or removed anywhere in the design.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if winograd_dsp_16 published its products untruncated (36-bit resulta/resultb instead of 16-bit) and winograd_adder_16_20_4 dropped its clken or gated data rather than the registers, the 112 winograd products could be regrouped two at a time into chained int_sop_2 blocks, with the blocks' chainout-to-chainin chains replacing the first two levels of each 16-input adder tree.
